ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u28	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! It has been my honor to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice concerning this bill. This law cannot be considered without a budget law. They're both closely connected. And I think it is necessary to remind, but I think some of us also clearly present a philosophy of budget creation by Law and Justice for 2017 as the first copyright budget of Law and Justice in this term. The philosophy of the budget, ladies and gentlemen, is clearly targeted, points to the citizen and family. Pro-family policy is the basis of this budget not only in its dry records, but also in the implementation of concrete projects about which literally in a moment. The second of these pillars is, of course, security, which is difficult not to talk about today, because those who are watching the situation, both internally and above all internationally, I think, must also focus attention on the security element. As I said, the budget is clearly focused on the citizen and the family. All the actions we take as Law and Justice in our policy are directed towards the citizen as an entity of our policy, not as a subject of government action, which has undoubtedly taken place in recent years. 40 billion PLN for family policies is probably a kind of very good record over the last 27 years, if we are looking at the finances of our country. Here you can include expenditure from the Labour Fund, which is expected to finance post-graduate internships and specialization training of doctors, dentists, nurses and midwives, in the amount of PLN 1 billion. It is also important to finance the membership contribution for Poland's participation in the Convention on the European Space Agency and to finance contributions allowing membership of other international organisations from the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. However, the most important provision of the Act concerns the redemption of loans granted to the Social Insurance Fund for the financing of benefits guaranteed by the State which are due on 31 March 2017. The debt of the Social Insurance Fund vis-à-vis the State Treasury for the loans granted from the State budget for the years 2009 and 2014, which are due to be repaid on 31 March, amounts to over 39 billion PLN. Instead of providing the Social Insurance Fund with a grant, the Minister of Finance of the previous government granted loans despite the awareness that they would never be repaid, except that the loan did not increase the deficit and the grant -yet. In this way, the public debt that the Civic Platform has been hiding for years, and which we now have to pay back. Over the years, the problem of the deficit has been swept under the carpet, but we are finally back to normal, and that's the end of creative accounting. About Free and Solidarni supports the proposed solution and will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The legislation provided for a new form of protection called the Heritage Treasury List to be conducted by the Minister of Culture and National Heritage. However, the Act did not enter into force because it was examined by the Constitutional Tribunal, which stated in its judgment of 25 May 2016 its compatibility with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which enabled the President to sign the Act on 16 August 2016. However, in the period between the adoption of the amendment and its signature by the President, the provisions have changed in such a way that it is necessary to amend it. Therefore, the draft amendment is mainly of a technical nature, necessary to ensure its consistency with the rules in force, including the preservation of many important regulatory monuments. According to the project, the Article was supplemented by the Act on the Protection of Monuments. 24a paragraph. 2 of that law, indicating which movable items in the event of their loss may be entered in the national register of lost cultural objects. The entry in this register is important for the preservation of ownership of lost movable property by the rightful owner, because it does not allow the acquisition of such movable property by another entity in good faith. Considering that the monuments included in the Heritage Treasure List have special value for cultural heritage, their rightful owners, including museums, libraries and archives, should benefit from these legal solutions. The Act also lists the requirements for education and professional experience which must be met by persons conducting conservation works on monuments entered in the register of monuments, and indicates that these requirements will also have to be met by persons carrying out such activities on monuments entered on the list of Heritage Treasures. The Club of the Polish People's Party welcomes this regulation. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u70	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Government's draft law amending the law on the application for an infringement of the party's right to investigate a case in preparatory proceedings conducted or supervised by the prosecutor and judicial proceedings without undue delay and certain other laws, print No 851. The Sejm at its 27th meeting on 5 October 2016 pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights. The Commission, after considering this draft law at its meeting on 27 October 2016, requested the High Sejm to adopt the draft law contained in print No 964. The Commission, as set out in the Article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, shall, at the request of the applicant, Robert Kropiwnicki, submit a request from a minority to delete Article. 10 in the draft in print No 851. I'll remind you, Art. The 10 of the original draft was as follows: "For acts committed before the date of entry into force of this Act, the statute of limitations in the wording of this Act shall apply unless the limitation period has expired. High Izbo, seven amendments were introduced at the committee meeting on 27 October in the original draft. Most of them were complex. They included a few articles. As regards Amendment 1, this was the implementation of the recommendation of the Council of Ministers, which examined the draft at its meeting on 5 September 2016. It is a change concerning the powers of the State Treasury and the public finance sector. The draft original law provides that these entities will not be entitled to lodge a complaint. However, after analysis, it was considered that there were insufficient arguments for such exclusion. It is therefore proposed to delete in Article. Point 1 (3). However, it was considered that the granting of the sum of money should the action brought by the State Treasury or the State body be taken into account would not be justified, since the sum granted by the State Treasury would belong to the State Treasury, would be allocated to the State Treasury. It is also proposed to waive the proposed waiver of the obligation to justify the complaint. While the proposed amendments to the provisions relating to the complaint for the duration of proceedings introduce provisions to depart from the practice of fragmentation of proceedings by requiring the court hearing the application to take account of all its stages, the solution contained in the draft does not require the applicant to indicate in the complaint at which stage the proceedings took place would result in the obligation for the court competent to consider the complaint to provide a notice of pending proceedings to the Treasury, to the President of any court before which proceedings were pending, while providing him with a copy of the application. This would, on the one hand, increase the costs of proceedings and impose additional duties on judicial staff and, on the other hand, may extend the time taken to investigate the case. In addition, it is proposed to reduce, in relation to the proposed minimum amount, the sum of the monetary amount granted for each year of the proceedings up to PLN 500, while defining the conditions governing the possibility for the court to reach a higher amount. Indeed, it does not appear that it is justified, in particular, to leave the possibility of judging for the complainant an amount higher than PLN 500 for each year of proceedings in cases in which its faulty conduct would lead to an extension of the procedure, e.g. when the defendant was hiding in court proceedings where there was either a delay in the investigation of the case, or in a situation where the case affected by the chronicity is not of particular relevance from the complainant's point of view. Cases of particular relevance to the complainant according to the case law of the European Court of Human Rights are primarily labour law, social security and criminal matters. Clearly, these people's rights are often restricted in social security or labour law, and there should be an exceptional speed in these proceedings. In addition, in the article. 14 information was provided on the possibility of re-submitting a complaint in the event of its rejection by the court in view of the failure to complete the formal deficiencies within the time limit and that provision was divided into paragraphs. Consequently, the wording of the Article has been amended. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 referring to Article. 14. The charge on the complaint was also increased by PLN 100, which is intended to reduce the number of non-justifiable complaints which result in an extension of the proceedings. On the other hand, if the application referred to in paragraph is made. 1, has been lodged in appeal proceedings, the court of appeal, forwarding the case to the prosecutor in order to complete the investigation or investigation, repeals the judgment under appeal. Such an instrument granted to the prosecutor's office, which interferes with the powers of the court, is very dangerous, as with irresponsible application it will lead to the chronicity of court proceedings and general chaos in the justice system. The prosecution has a duty to prepare the full material before filing an indictment. The new essential circumstances can always form the basis for initiating a new proceeding and therefore the solution adopted by the applicant is surprising. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u100	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the report of the Communication Commission with Poles abroad from the examination of the draft law on the establishment of the Eastern Cross, print No 889. Ladies and gentlemen, this law provides for the establishment of a new state award for foreigners, who in the years 1937 and 1959 very often risked their own lives and their loved ones' lives to save our compatriots at the borders of I and II of the Republic of Poland. These border dates are very important. The first of them, 1937, is the beginning of large repressions in the Soviet Union, it is the so-called. operation of the Polish NKVD, within which more than 111 thousand. Poles were shot and tens of thousands were subjected to repression. This was also the result of the uprisings, camps, and after 17 September, after the attack of the Soviet Union on the Second Republic, similar repressions, similar sufferings were subjected to our compatriots living in the eastern voivodeship of the Second Republic. Later came the year 1943 and genocide, which was carried out by Ukrainian nationalists in Poles living in Volyn and south-eastern voivodships. There were also dramatic events when collaborating Lithuanian and Belarusian troops participated in the murder of Poles. Our countrymen were killed because they were Poles. However, in these confusions of evil, in these tragic times it was possible to find people who, risking their lives, helped their Polish neighbors. The Polish state has never thanked these people so far. So the establishment of this distinction is a kind of repayment of moral debt to all those who have taken such risks, who have shown humanity in these dramatic times. The establishment of this distinction is also a call for the heritage of the limits and all that is connected with it, a call for multiculturalism, tolerance and fraternal life of nations, which later in the 20th century had such a dramatic finale. Three substantive amendments and several editorial and legislative amendments were tabled at the committee meeting on 4 October. These content concerns, among others, the possibility for the President to deprive the distinction of persons who behave in an abusive manner towards the Republic of Poland or Poles. The proposed change in the image of the cross was modified by adding Chrobre's crown and proposed to change the date of entry into force of the law to 1 January 2017. The Communication Commission with Poles abroad after first reading and considering this project, together with the amendments, invites the High Sejm to adopt the draft. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u134	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the Government's draft law amending the Trade Quality Act on agri-food products, contained in print No 957, and the draft law to which it relates is print No 792. The first reading of the project took place in the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on 4 October, followed by a subcommittee, whose report was examined by the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on 21 October and adopted unanimously. The aim of the amendment is to introduce the possibility of voluntary labelling of food products with information about the Polish product along with the identification of the design of the mark. It should be pointed out that the Polish Product mark will be used by itself, as well as the labels, by the marketing entity of the agri-food article concerned. The correct use of this mark by traders will be subject to inspection of the Quality of Trade Inspection of Agricultural and Food Articles, as is currently controlled by the composition of the product described on the label. So what conditions must the trader fulfil? In the case of a unprocessed product, the Polish product can use the mark, if the primary production took place in the territory of the Republic of Poland, and in the case of meat - if it was additionally obtained from animals born in Poland and their breeding and slaughter took place in the territory of Poland. In the case of processed products, the label can be used if these products are manufactured in the territory of the Republic of Poland from ingredients manufactured in Poland, with the use of imported raw materials up to 25% by weight of the final product, provided that they are not present or cannot be manufactured in Poland. As a result of the adoption of this amendment, consumers will be able to search for those products that were produced in Poland from native raw materials on the shelves. The vast majority of consumers expect such solutions. Similar solutions have already been applied in some countries of the European Union. At this point, it is worth noting that this amendment is in line with European Union law. During the work of the Agriculture Committee, the Kukiz Club submitted two amendments, but they were not supported by a majority of Members. The proposed changes are expected by most consumers and food producers in Poland, both farmers and entrepreneurs, so on behalf of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development I would like the High Sejm to adopt a government bill amending the act on the commercial quality of agri-food products. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u135	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Krzysztof Szulowski will be the first to speak on behalf of Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u136	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club on the proposed amendment of the Act of 21 December 2000 on the commercial quality of agri-food products, printing Nos 792 and 957. The project concerns the introduction of new regulations on the voluntary labelling of foodstuffs with information about the Polish product, together with the definition of the design of the graphic symbol to be used when indicating such information. Until now, the law has not regulated the issue of labelling foodstuffs with information about the country of origin. The introduction of clear criteria enabling voluntary information to be provided by the Polish Product will enable interested consumers to search for products manufactured in Poland using Polish raw materials. This is to meet the needs of both consumers interested in purchasing products manufactured in the country and producers who would like to provide information on the national origin of the product produced both with regard to the place of production and with regard to the ingredients used. At present, consumers are very often unaware that a product, which they regard as a Polish product because of its brand, does not really have much in common with this term because it was produced from foreign ingredients, e.g. meat from animals not only unborn in Poland, but even here not bred. However, the need for information on the origin of a number of food products is high among consumers. The proposed changes are designed to meet these expectations and prevent abuse. The use of an unambiguously sound term "Polish product" will mean to the consumer that it can be sure that the product has been manufactured in the country and the raw materials for its production are of national origin. In the case of unprocessed agri-food products, such a product must meet the following criteria: the product is not processed, although of course it can be split, ground, frozen, etc., production, cultivation or breeding, including harvesting, milking in the case of cows, sheep and goats, took place in the territory of Poland. In the case of meat, it is additionally required that it be obtained from animals born in Poland and their breeding and slaughter took place in the territory of our country and, in the case of non-meat products of animal origin, that it be obtained from animals raised in the territory of Poland. In the case of agri-food products, the following conditions are processed to meet: processing took place in the territory of Poland from raw materials or produced in Poland, other than those indicated above, must not exceed 25% the total weight of all ingredients at the time of their use for the manufacture of this product, excluding the water used, and those components cannot be replaced by the same ingredients which were produced in Poland. Given that it is now possible to market graphical signs with information from the Polish product developed by individual manufacturers to harmonise the transmission of information to consumers and prevent confusion, a one-year transitional period for the marketing of foodstuffs, which are marked with a graphic mark containing information from the Polish product which does not comply with the model set out in the implementing rules of the Act, even if the product fulfils the conditions set out in the Act for the posting of information. The transitional period aims to prevent food waste and the use of packaging labelled before the proposed law enters into force, while at the same time preventing the trading of different graphical characters containing information about the Polish product. Compliance with the proposed legislation on voluntary labelling of agri-food products with the information "Polish product" will be subject to control of the commercial quality carried out by the Agricultural and Food Quality Inspection. Polish food is characterized by very good quality and other qualities from other countries. There are many companies producing in Poland that would like to boast about this and stand out against the background of other manufacturers. The origin of the product is a very important factor in consumer choices. The amendment of these provisions will benefit both producers and buyers. The act will be part of strengthening the consumer patriotism of Poles. Therefore, the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development deserves to be commended for the drafting of this bill, and the Law and Justice Club will of course support it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u144	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The position of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club towards this project is relatively positive, i.e. We believe that this project should be processed. This project, on the other hand, seems to have more disadvantages than advantages in our assessment. The speakers have already talked about this. It's pretty positive, I mean. you had good intentions, but the execution does not fully confirm or express those intentions. Of course, we observe such trends in Poland and in Europe, we often call them consumer patriotism, regional patriotism, local patriotism, where citizens want to eat food from a given area, produced in a given country, from where they live. Such a trend is also seen in EU legislation, because we have several signs that speak about the protection of geographical indications or designations of origin. However, if we refer to this patriotism, it is also worth paying attention to what, first of all - whether in Poland or in another European country or in the world - the consumer is guided by entering the shop or the place of sale, choosing the product. Well, first of all, he decides to buy this product, given the price. This is the first parameter that's taken into account. Only in second place is the quality parameter. On the third other parameters, e.g. visual, somewhere far away taste, and at the end of which country this product comes from. But this is due to our positive assessment of intent. However, this trend is worth changing, clearly to the consumer, what it eats. Besides, we can give an example when Russia introduced an embargo, there was a nationwide action supporting Polish Sadists, an action of eating apples. And that really translated into an increase in consumption, but for a month or two or three. Today everyone has forgotten about it and everyone, going to the store, chooses apples first, which are cheaper and not those which are Polish. And we win this competition because we offer apples cheaper. And I think that's all about the good intentions. Now, on the other hand, what are the drawbacks of this project. First of all, this is an incorrect regulation, i.e. it causes that on the side can be sold under this symbol products of unknown origin, from unknown raw materials, of unknown quality. Because the control system which under this law is entrusted with the Agricultural and Food Quality Inspection is leaky, because the inspection does not have so many officials, nor so many inspectors, nor such structures, so that it can effectively control whether the sausage is only made from half-carcases coming from a Polish pig or sometimes not from a Danish pig in a given meat plant. Because they hang alternately on the hooks in the freezer, and only later do they go to the sausage production. Or even bread - we are not able to tell today whether it will be made from flour originating in Poland or from flour from Ukraine. And no inspector of the commercial quality of agri-food will be able to prove it or deny it. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u148	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the government draft law amending the Act on Commercial Quality of Agricultural and Food Products. The draft law deals with the possibility of additional labelling of agri-food products with a graphic symbol containing the information of the Polish product and regulates the formal conditions for such a product. Poles like Polish agri-food products and believe that they are better, healthier and tastier than produced abroad with highly specialised industrial methods aimed at minimizing production costs, often at the price of lower quality parameters. Such an opinion about Polish products is also common outside our country. Currently, the situation in terms of naming Polish products is very ambiguous and can often be misleading. This applies in particular to processed products. It is enough that the imported product is packaged or slightly modified by the Polish producer, and can already be described as a Polish product. [[It's okay.]] It is worth noting that the Polish product is visible and unambiguous, so that it is clear what it means and what the buyer guarantees. Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle support initiatives aimed at easier access of customers to information supporting producers of products from Polish raw materials in production plants located in Poland and initiatives that will easily allow informed choice of products purchased, especially since this marking is not mandatory and introduced voluntarily by the manufacturer. At the same time, I would like to mention two examples that illustrate how difficult it is to create a good clear law, effective and good for citizens. As defined in the amendment of the law, we cannot name an orange drink with 30% orange juice content, but an orange drink with 25% juice content can already be a Polish product. In this way, the amendment can contribute to a deterioration of the quality of the product. The second example is related to meat production. Now meat production is very often done, let's call it this, in the technological process, where animals are one of the raw materials. What is the effect that meat will come from animals grown, slaughtered and undressed in Poland if the second raw material in the production process is imported feed, additionally containing plant ingredients from genetically modified plants? Will it also be a Polish product? Probably similar examples can be cited more, so it is worth rethinking the definitions contained in the amendment, whether these definitions should be included in the law in this form. Would it not be better to introduce more detailed provisions in the relevant regulation of the competent minister? Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! There is one other question that is not raised in the amendment - what about products that do not comply with the requirements of the Act, but their producers will want to use the indication that it is a Polish product? It is clear that producers will not be able to use a proprietary logo, but it is no longer obvious that the use of a similar mark or an own mark of such content is prohibited. We appreciate this legislative initiative as being intended to protect the market of Polish agri-food producers, but in order for the draft law already to have the intended effect, it is necessary to specify in detail which product, by which product, under which conditions, and from which components will be marked as a Polish product benefiting from legal protection. On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I request that the bill be further examined in committee. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u256	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The main objective of the revision of the Law on public-funded healthcare provision, as well as the revision of other laws, was and is to restore funding from the state budget of vaccines to carry out compulsory preventive vaccinations. Therefore, the National Health Fund's commitment to finance the purchase of those vaccines was to be waived on 1 January 2017. This will free up the funds at the disposal of the national fund with the prospect of their intended use for health services. In particular, this applies to the appropriations already foreseen in the 2017 budget of the NFZ. This change is also part of the implementation of the State's responsibility for health policy, i.e. the protection of public health. In the course of the committee's work, amendments to the Pharmaceutical Law Act, which were intended to enable the acquisition by new operators of vaccines to carry out the recommended preventive vaccinations, were discontinued. Let's add right away that they gave up the right thing to do. The marketing of vaccines should be subject to appropriate regulation, and in the present case the proposed rules did not incorporate a new category of vaccine operators into the current regime. The right idea must therefore look forward to a more precise solution, thus a solution separate from this amendment. This resignation was the biggest change made to the government project. For the sake of order, it is also acceptable to say that the amendment contained in prints 890 and 960, although it does not abolish the obligation to confirm the right to benefits, provides that the National Health Fund will not investigate the costs of basic healthcare benefits if there are problems with confirming the right to benefits. This does not apply to the costs incurred for the purchase of medicines, special purpose foodstuffs and medical devices. These costs will be incurred by the fund on the basis of existing rules. As a consequence, the draft contains provisions for the death of the administrative decisions in question and for the death of enforcement proceedings relating to those entitlements. This draft introduces provisions allowing for the retroactive notification to health insurance, although only within certain deadlines, it also provides for the release of providers from the obligation to transmit data from waiting lists to the health information system. These issues were discussed in detail at first reading and do not require a broader discussion here. The Health Committee requests the adoption of a draft law in print No 960. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u268	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position towards the Government's draft law amending the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds and certain other laws. The main objective of the project is to restore funding for vaccines to carry out compulsory preventive vaccinations from the state budget. Through the changes made, the state will again take responsibility for health policy, where preventive action is one of the most important places. In the justification of the projectors we can read that protective vaccinations are part of the activity carried out by the State in accordance with Article. 68 constitutions and serve to protect public health. There is no need for a better justification for the validity of this change than the implementation of the constitutional rights of Poles. The bill is intended to ensure that any person who benefits from primary healthcare benefits does not bear the costs of providing health care even if there are difficulties in confirming his/her right to healthcare. I agree with the projectors that this will eliminate situations in which the patient has not benefited from benefits for fear of costs. Often, avoiding the use of aid has generated additional costs for the health care system, as specialist treatment has already been needed. An important change also concerns the non-punishment of those citizens who, although fulfilling the requirements of being insured, have failed to comply with the obligation to apply for insurance. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u289	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to present a government bill amending the retail tax law contained in print No.952. The Act of 6 July 2016 introduced a new tax on retail sales into the Polish tax system. Its adoption was preceded by extensive public consultations and met with acceptance of most Polish traders. The declared objective of introducing a new tax, in addition to increasing the tax revenue of the state budget, is to offset competition in the retail market. However, by decision of 19 September 2016, the European Commission initiated a detailed investigation into our tax. The Commission is concerned that progressive revenue-based rates confer a selective advantage on low-income entrepreneurs over their competitors, in breach of EU State aid rules. At the same time, the European Commission has ordered Poland to suspend the application of progressive tax rates until a decision on the compatibility of the law with the internal market has been taken. I would like to stress that the European Commission's allegations regarding the non-compliance of our legislation with EU law are without strong substantive arguments, constituting a fairly biased interpretation of the EU rules on State aid. For these reasons, we are now at the stage of making an application to the Court of Justice of the European Union for a suspension order. Since the settlement of the dispute on the possibility of introducing the tax in the proposed form will take place before the Court of Justice of the European Union and there is a risk of financial consequences for Poland in the event of a unfavourable settlement of the dispute, the government has taken the initiative to introduce a temporary solution. Firstly, on 18 October the Minister for Development and Finance issued a regulation on the cessation of the collection of retail tax, including the tax due from September to December of that year, which would allow the effective expiry of the tax obligations for that purpose. At the same time, the Council of Ministers has drawn up this draft law which suspends the application of the Retail Tax Act until the end of 2017. This is the optimal solution. On the one hand, Poland implements the Commission's recommendation while protecting the budget against any payment of interest on the tax collected. On the other hand, we do not fully repeal the tax before the final decision is taken by the European Commission. That would be premature. Poland does not capitulate or anticipate the Commission's decision. We have serious and factual arguments in the dispute with Brussels. The adoption of this Act will not have negative consequences for taxable persons who are no longer obliged to pay the tax due and will in addition not have to fulfil other tax obligations under tax law. In addition, press reports on the negative practices of certain trading networks that passed the cost of tax on suppliers and producers are of a unit nature and only point to the calculation of those networks. At the same time, I would like to stress that there will be no negative consequences for the state budget due to the possible payment of interest on the tax collected. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The Polish government is on the side of honest entrepreneurs operating on our territory. We do not discriminate against companies with foreign capital, as they try to show some foreign trading networks. All retailers who earn a monthly income of more than PLN 17 million are subject to tax, and this does not apply solely or mainly to foreign entities. The European Commission's response to our tax is contrary to other officially declared actions to support small and medium-sized enterprises. The Commission is actually defending those who benefit from economies of scale rather than supporting those who do not have that possibility. And it does so in a discriminatory way in the context of unqualified trade taxes in France and Spain. The proposed time limit for suspension of the application of the law should allow the Commission to take a final decision on the compatibility of the tax with State aid rules. We are in constant contact with the representatives of the Commission. We explain to them the issues at issue regarding our tax and discuss the inaccuracies contained in the notified decision. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! By passing the bill to the High House for consideration, I hope that it will be accepted with understanding and that work on it will proceed smoothly. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u290	Thank you, Minister. I would like to ask Mrs Paulina Hennig-Kloska to provide the reasons for the bill in print No 928. You're welcome, my fellow Member.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u291	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The purpose of the bill, which is contained in print No 928, is to repeal the provisions of the Act of 6 July 2016 on retail tax, which the Minister mentioned just now. The law, let us remind you, imposes additional tax burdens on the owners of retail outlets. In our opinion, however, this is an indirect way of extracting money from the pockets of Poles. Today no one really has any doubt that this new tax pays or will pay customers, suppliers and subcontractors of commercial networks, not themselves. Retail trade in Poland is characterised by quite low margins. According to the CSO in 2015, the average net profitability in this industry is 1.8%. Even before the new tribute came into force, merchants began to look for funds to cover it. This first affected the profits of producers supplying goods to taxable shops. Some networks have sent official letters to their suppliers asking for additional discounts, others have done so in a more meticulous way, but many Polish companies - producers of hedges, farmers, transport companies, security companies or cleaners - had to sign new terms of cooperation based on new prices already a few months ago. Today, the tax was suspended, but no one to those Polish companies who had already absorbed it into their costs did not give it away. He has not given and will not give back, especially in a situation of uncertainty, whether and when, in what amount and under what conditions this tax will return. Today, this uncertainty is driven by Polish trade, new investments in commercial areas, the creation of new jobs in Polish trade, and this uncertainty is pushed by nobody else, like the irresponsible government of PiS. It is also worth mentioning once again that this tax will not protect small neighborhood shops. They'll still be much more expensive than in the markets and customers won't change their habits. It's a lie pushed by the PiS easily overturned by the facts. And they look like the difference in the margin between hypermarkets and housing stores is often up to 20% today and will not change that 1.5 percent tax. Small shops have to bet on proximity, availability, quality, freshness or the provision of additional services, not the price. It is also worth recalling that the tax will hit the poorest Poles most, because it is they who shop at food discounts, which according to the latest proposal paid the highest rates. As soon as the law was passed, they started a very fierce fight for customer retention, which could be observed by giving up the fight in the form of competition for the direct price of the product. They implemented various loyalty programs, which were intended to divert attention from the price, while focusing the attention of customers on themselves, often at the expense of these small Polish neighborhood shops. Taxes must be created so that they give some predictability, a sense of stability to investors who want to invest their money in Poland. Today, this predictability and stability is completely lacking. The form of trade taxation in the course of the year has been worked over at least a few and the end cannot be seen. If you can't create taxes, don't create taxes. Should we be surprised that the tax has been suspended? We have already said in the course of the bill that the tax is not in line with European Union law that the European Commission will question its progressive nature, as in Hungary. PiS politicians knew that, too, and yet they did not prepare plan B. You've gone into chaos and incompetence because chaos and incompetence is your middle name. No one was surprised by the European Commission's communication of 19 September this year about the initiation of a detailed investigation into the retail tax in Poland, but the Minister had the opportunity to comment extensively on the commercial lobby in the European Union. I therefore wonder whether, in this situation, you will be blaming the European Union for not fulfilling your promises before the next election campaign. I have just a slight remark: don't forget that your promises cost about PLN 100 billion a year, and this tax was supposed to pay PLN 1.5 billion. Retail tax in the form adopted in the Act of 6 July this year. may also be contrary to Article. 32 Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which guarantees equality before the law for all. The continuation of the Act may lead to market segmentation and affect the decisions of economic operators on the business undertaken and conducted. The tax can, of course, be contrary to the EU ban on unlawful state aid. However, the authors of the law claim, as the Minister said in front of me, that tax preferences for small shops fall within EU law, because under certain conditions it allows the use of public aid for the small and medium-sized enterprises sector. However, EU documents do not refer to general tax preferences as an acceptable form of aid to SMEs, but merely to the fact that such aid must have a specific objective. If you don't know what a specific goal is, I'll tell you: it could be. support for innovation, vocational activation of women, support for investments or environmental measures, rather than simply introducing a new tribute aimed solely at the fiscal objective. This issue has led to the abolition of a very similar tax in Hungary, and you know that too. The progressive tax had to be replaced by a linear tax because the European Commission considered it contrary to European Union law. The Minister has assured us here that you will fight for this tax at the Court, but let's see what this has been like in the Hungarian case already known to us. From October 2010 to January 2013, Hungary was in force so-called. a crisis tax. It was based on revenues of companies from retail, energy and telecommunications industries. The basis was revenue and it was progressive, with a tax-free amount. Progression - from 0.1 to 2,5% revenue. Under the pressure of the Court of Justice, to which you wish to complain to the European Commission's decision, the Hungarian Government withdrew from this tax. In 2014, the so-called "Hungary" appeared in Hungary. the fee for official food control charged on sale in grocery stores. The fee is progressive, from 0 to 6%. In July 2015, this tax was suspended due to doubts raised by the European Commission. The Commission has accused unlawful public aid and unequal treatment. The progress of the tax has led companies with high margins and low turnover to a better situation than those with low margins and high turnover. In its reply in November 2015, Hungary changed the formula of the linear tax to a very low tax rate - 0,1% revenue. Hungarian supermarket taxes had negative economic effects in the form of exemptions and closures, restrictions on investment in commercial space and increased food prices. Since for you the Hungarian example is worthy of following on many other issues, I completely do not understand why you do not want to learn from these mistakes and those effects that you already see. Ladies and gentlemen, returning to the tax in Poland - today we have such a state that the European Commission has issued an order obliging Poland to suspend the application of this tax until the analysis is completed. We are threatened by proceedings before the European Court of Justice. The government hasn't come up with a plan B that we've been asking for for for a long time. The present form of the proposed tax, like any other one, is harmful to the economy, and is nothing more than an attempt to draw money out of taxpayers' pockets, because it translates directly in proportion to the prices of goods or is translated into production costs for food producers. Therefore, it is appropriate not to suspend this tax, but simply to abolish it, so as to end the entire farce associated with the failure of the Government of Law and Justice to implement this tax. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u301	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The European Commission has taken a decision ordering Poland to suspend the granting of unlawful aid until a final decision has been taken by the Commission in which it would presumably consider that the provisions of the Act are compatible with the internal market. However, if the Commission's final decision were negative, it may be necessary to reimburse this aid, which would put the State Treasury at risk of loss. In view of the above, the project envisages a shift in the validity of the Retail Tax Act from sales revenue achieved from 1 January 2018 and therefore the application of the Act to retail sales revenue achieved between September 2016 and December 2017 is suspended. Let me remind you that the main purpose of the retail tax was to strengthen the position of small shops at the expense of supermarkets run by foreign capital. It was intended to increase the chances of Polish entrepreneurs and burden foreign trade networks. This is what the government has convinced us to do by introducing this tax. Polish entrepreneurs, farmers, supply companies, delivery companies, security companies and even cleaning workers suffered mostly. The managers of large trading networks, with a strong negotiating advantage - as could be expected - came to their suppliers with an expectation of applying supply discounts to cover the costs of the tax introduced. Shouldn't the current retail tax failure be supported by the second draft, from print No. 928, namely the draft repealing the Retail Tax Act of 6 July 2016? We believe that such a decision would be the most rational and most legitimate. Designed solutions are essential to ensure that all women waiting for their offspring feel safe and help from the state in the event of a diagnosis of irreversibly impaired or incurable illness in their expected child. It is therefore extremely important for the country to include women experiencing pregnancy pathology or expecting a sick, disabled child with proper care and support. It is necessary to run an aid scheme for families expecting a sick child. State care in the area envisaged by this project is intended to include access to healthcare services, including prenatal diagnostics. The envisaged support for families and waiting women will be implemented through access to information on support solutions. Both mother and child will have access to adequate healthcare services. In addition, the project provides that women expecting a sick child and their families will receive a cash benefit granted by the State. Of course, the money provided by the government to the families waiting for the sick child will not reduce their suffering, but the support granted will help prepare the family better for the reception of the sick child. The existing aid in this area is not sufficient. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u348	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government's draft Act on the support of pregnant women and their families. The aim of the proposed solutions is to regulate comprehensive support for pregnant women and their families, with particular emphasis on complicated pregnant women and in the event of maternity failure. The forms of support shall include: ensuring access to information on support solutions, prenatal diagnostics and the possibility of giving birth in hospital in the third degree of reference. Families are also expected to have access to broad guidance on family support solutions, overcoming difficulties in caring and raising a child, access to social and occupational rehabilitation and health care services. Within the framework of these benefits, the family will receive medical, nursing, psychological and access to rehabilitation. Care under the Act will be implemented by domestic hospices, as well as in special cases by stationary hospices, as well as in the care plant. The Act introduces special preferences for families with children who have been diagnosed with severe and irreversible disability or incurable illness that threatens their lives. They relate to the use of healthcare services and pharmaceutical services provided in pharmacies, palliative and hospice care of children outside the order. The bill is a very important step towards changes that facilitate the daily functioning of families with children who have been diagnosed with severe and irreversible disability or incurable illness threatening their lives, arising during the prenatal period of the child's development or during childbirth. No one needs to be convinced that support for women in complicated pregnancy and families with disabled children is needed. During today's work in the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs, there were many allegations that the aid was too small that the project was understated that it was too fast to consult non-governmental organisations. I agree that the public consultation, as well as the consultation with the most interested families who raise such children, would be desirable. However, it must be stressed that the project is to help those who expect this assistance as soon as possible and fill the gap that has existed over the years. It's true, it's $4,000. PLN offered one-time assistance is not much, but it is more than anyone has ever offered to these families. Besides, I am convinced that the government's announcements that this is only the first stage of helping these particularly experienced families are true. I hope that the expected changes, changes in the whole family system, will be staged by the Law and Justice Government. It welcomes what the Minister of Labour and Social Policy announces, that the project is widely consulted between the resorts, so its final shape will comprehensively address all the needs of children with disabilities. Projectors stress that an individual approach to the needs of individual families, which is to be provided by a specially trained family assistant, will be of particular importance. A very important solution is undoubtedly the creation of an informant on the rights under this project. Due to the fact that the contested law is to support Polish families, giving them a sense of security and allowing them better protection of the state, the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle asks for further proceding of the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

In Germany, after primary school, which lasts from 4 to 6 years, depending on the state, students have a very diverse educational offer and can take up school, depending on their abilities, in junior high school or Realschule and Hauptschule. This means that most German students are prepared at an early stage to gain a useful profession. Currently, the Polish system deviates from this model and is in fact a 2-level or even 1-level system, given that often the profession is achieved only after graduating from higher education. It is better to have a high-tech economy based on high professional qualifications than good results in international tests and a low-innovation economy. Each educational system is a complex distributed system in which very many social processes occur. Attempts to introduce new regulations must therefore be met with criticism of interest groups operating under the current system. A strategic analysis of the education sector is a major challenge, as it requires an agreement of a large number of stakeholders, actors and stakeholders in the system. Therefore, it is necessary to continue social dialogue on this fundamental issue for the future of Poland. The assumptions of the basic education system should be based on the principle of competing on a regulated educational market for different actors and, on the other hand, competing for the best personal development pathways in schools that meet their needs. Such a structured education system would feed the economy and society into the best-prepared professional staff capable of addressing today's challenges in global goods and services markets. As Members of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, we will support Minister Anna Zalewska in this necessary reform, however, hoping that the target shape of the Polish educational system will result from an agreement of all political forces and will be based on a deep strategic analysis and very well formulated objectives. The education system should be built on the principle of social solidarity and mutual respect and on the professionalism of teaching staff. The building of social ties and a system of values at school must be based on education in the spirit of patriotism and the promotion of responsible attitudes. We are convinced that Minister Zalewska is guided by these values in the implementation of education reform. Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle will vote against the vote of distrust for the Minister of National Education, Ms Anna Zalewska. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u463	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Today, High Izbo, the first reading took place and the bill was examined. The committee meeting was a long, comprehensive meeting on the subject covered by the draft law. The changes in the feed law are not extensive, they concern only two articles, that is literally a few sentences. During today's debate, the draft was presented by the Minister, was widely discussed, and then there was quite a rich discussion. What's the deal with this project? The point is to postpone the deadline for the following years and replace it in the article. 1 and Article 1 65 of that law, the words "January 1st 2017" are replaced by the words "January 1st 2019." High Izbo, the point is to dismiss the time limit for the prohibition on the use in the production of feed of genetically modified organisms intended for feed use. A few words about the story, where the problem came from, the subject. Well, High Izbo, in July 2006, the then Sejm passed the Law on Feed, in which it stated that it had been banned and prohibited from producing, placing on the market and feeding genetically modified feedingstuffs and genetically modified organisms within 2 years of the entry into force of that Law. When two years passed in July 2008, there was a problem. I was already an envoy by then. I remember the debate on this topic about the government project of the then coalition of PO and PSL. It was then proposed to postpone this ban by 4 years until July 2012. It was then announced that a special program of substitution of protein, which comes from genetically modified plants, protein from non-modified plants, which is produced in Poland, would be developed by the government. Unfortunately, this deadline was not met, and the same problem again emerged in 2012. In fact, the introduction of this ban was postponed by more than four years - it was on 1 January 2017. Today, for similar reasons, we are approaching a shift of this term, only not by 4 years, but by 2 years. During today's committee deliberations, as I said earlier, there was a broad discussion on this subject. There were many people, institutions and associations who said that such a restriction should be prohibited, but also that there should not be such a ban. There were various arguments: health, economic account, necessity or need to maintain high production, good production of Polish agriculture in the production of poultry meat, where the genetically modified plants in the feed additive are used. There were votes in favour, there were votes against. As in the consultation that the project was subject to, there were such voices: some environments were in favour of not extending this deadline to ban the production of feed from genetically modified organisms and plants, and others were in favour of maintaining this possibility. As a result of a broad discussion at today's meeting of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, a draft law was drawn up at first reading, which was included in print No 978. During the vote, members of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development practically unanimously voted in favour of the fact that the High Sejm attached in this print report No. 978, the draft law amending the Law on Feed, which I am proposing on behalf of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development. I ask the High House to adopt this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

In Poland, we produce about 8.5 million tons of feed, and for this production we consume more than 2 million GM soybean buckwheat, which is the main protein component. We spend PLN 3 to 4 billion a year on this import. I'm saying they could grow, of course it's colloquialism. They're already growing, but they're not growing enough. The implementation takes place between 2016 and 2020 and has been allocated almost PLN 34 million. Professor Rutkowski stressed that the awareness of farmers in other aspects should also be increased. Such and other proposed solutions and guidelines cannot necessarily produce effects from day to day or even from month to month, and will entail specific costs, but we must ask ourselves: Isn't Polish agriculture really worth fighting for its high quality and proven brand? ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u506	Thank you very much. The discussion is closed. We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u507	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a bill amending certain laws to facilitate the sale of food by farmers. The project aims or aims at creating better opportunities for the development of production and sales to final consumers, final consumers of food produced on the farm in whole or in part from their own cultivation, farming or farming. So far, we have been dealing, as you probably know, with various other forms, in other words, so-called. direct sales. It really was several different types of sales, it was direct sales, direct deliveries, limited production, local, marginal and was also retail, and now we are introducing agricultural retail. In order not to talk too long, I would like to say this: in order to introduce this possibility of retailing related to the processing of food produced on the farm, we had to propose here a revision of seven laws, i.e.: Laws on the departments of government administration, the Act on income tax on individuals, the Act on flat-rate income tax on certain revenues, then the Act on commercial quality of agri-food products, the Act on Veterinary Inspection, the Act on products of animal origin, as well as the Act on food safety and nutrition. What's so important about this bill? First of all, we point out the competence of the authorities that will supervise the safety of food produced in the framework of agricultural retail trade. In this respect, We have proposed that veterinary inspection, the processing and production of food of animal origin, the sanitary inspection, and the Inspection of Commercial Quality of Agricultural and Food Articles, be carried out on the production of food of animal origin and produced from mixed raw materials, i.e. plant and animal origin, should be supervised by veterinary inspection, on the processing and production of food of plant origin. Of course, we are talking here about three inspections, because for today, in the legal system that exists, there is no new inspection that we are talking about and which is planned to be created from 2018. For today, the situation is that these three inspections will be supervised, but in the future it will be one consolidated inspection which will take place under supervision of the production of this food. The Minister for Agriculture, in agreement with the Minister for Health, will issue a regulation for the purposes of this law, which will regulate the quantitative limits of food to be produced on the farm. Tax rules will also be laid down. In general, tax preferences will be introduced for agricultural producers engaged in retail trade, taking into account inter alia. cases where the slaughter of animals for placing on the market, i.e. for trade, will have to take place in the slaughterhouse, while the production of flour will have to take place at the mill. The principle is that production is to be carried out by its own forces, non-industrial methods on the farm, but it is exceptionally allowed, because this is an obligation, for example, for hygienic reasons, to slaughter animals in the slaughterhouse. Here, this is the exception that does not result in the loss of tax exemption. With regard to hygiene requirements, of course, the Minister of Agriculture will be able to issue a regulation, he will be able to apply certain derogations in terms of construction, certain rules of the organisation, while with regard to hygiene, hygiene requirements, as regards microbiological requirements of a particularly large field of manoeuvre, there is no room for manoeuvre, as EU rules apply in this respect. I would like to say this: this law has been expected by farmers for several years, it has been proposed to introduce various solutions, including the Kukiz club law, while the government has developed its project, which we are presenting to you right now. As far as tax solutions are concerned, the farmer will be able to sell the food produced on the farm up to 20 000. PLN will be exempt from tax, while after exceeding this amount or the limits set out in the regulation it will be able to report to the tax office the desire to be taxed on a flat-rate tax of 2%, but, of course, to benefit from this, it will be underlined, it will have to produce its own forces and non-industrial methods. That's what it looks like to a general extent. If there's any questions, I'd be happy to answer them.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u514	Thank you very much. I would like to ask Mrs Ewa Lieder to make a statement on behalf of the Modern Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u515	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The current rules governing the marketing and processing of food set out three levels for the implementation of this activity. The first is industrial processing and marketing plants without any area and quantitative restrictions. The second is the production of food as part of a marginal, local and limited activity, commonly called MOL. The third is so-called. direct sales from agricultural holdings. While the first two are functioning properly, the direct sales activity in practice is not functioning and the reason is the legal regulations designed so that they do not allow the economic balance of such activities. On the one hand, we have requirements related to hygiene and food safety, often set at a level that is not adequate to the real risks, and on the other hand, restrictions on sales opportunities which do not allow sales to cover the costs of meeting the conditions laid down by the Veterinary Inspection and the State Sanitary Inspection. It is therefore necessary to amend national law in order to make it possible to sell directly from agricultural holdings. The possibilities for changes to national law are limited by the need to comply with European Union law. The Union regulations allow national regulations other than the Union, provided that the area of trade is restricted to the local market and quantitative limits are set and that the manufacturer can be identified. The production and sale of food products in Poland is subject to a number of legal regulations, which are defined both by national law and by the legal documents of the European Union. These regulations concern both taxation and registration and safety of foodstuffs, raw materials, intermediate products and products used for animal and vegetable production placed on the market. There are as many as five legal acts regulating the production and marketing of food which affect the regulation of direct sales. I'm not going to replace them. The Law amending certain laws to facilitate the sale of food by farmers proposed by the government will make this sale less easier. Determination of a tax-free amount of 20 000. zł has no financial significance, only unnecessary complicates regulations. Exemption from 2% flat-rate tax of 20 000 PLN is the amount of 400 PLN of savings per year. In addition, it reduces the de minimis aid limit, which is not high for Poland. In addition, this requires changes in several laws. Amendments to the rules governing the sale of food by farmers are needed but should be carried out after consultation with farmers interested in such a form of activity so as not to multiply the rules which remain theoretical. Incomprehensible is the introduction of a low limit - 50% - the share of own production. It's supposed to be helping farmers, not traders. It is reasonable to allow farmers to use the services of slaughter and post-mortem treatment of animals, the production of groats, flour and flakes, but this should be complemented by other services, such as squeezing fruit and vegetable juices. What farmers expect and need is the possibility of processing and selling products from their own farm. This sale must be possible on the local market, without limiting production and markets. The basis is the possibility of selling at local commercial and catering outlets selling to the final consumer. The restrictions on production must take into account such turnover as to cover the necessary costs related to food safety regulations. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 20 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1c the return from own cultivation, farming or farming was deleted, i.e. trade in plant and animal products was allowed. This is far too far-reaching a change, allowing unfair competition against other traders. Only agricultural producers offering their own production should be exempt from the tax in the context of agricultural production. The Act creates a new concept: agricultural retail trade, which will create additional legal chaos. At the same time, it does not remove barriers to the development of direct sales from agricultural holdings. Chaos will also bring about changes in competence in the Veterinary Inspection and the State Sanitary Inspection. In conclusion, it is not appropriate to introduce the proposed amendments. Work needs to be done on changes to the rules governing direct sales. Despite our various comments, the project is very much needed, and farmers and consumers are looking forward to it. Therefore, the Modern Posel Club is in favour of directing this amendment to further legislative work. Thank you very much.

In the case of the production and placing on the market of foodstuffs of non-animal origin in the framework of agricultural retail trade, the supervision of these activities will remain the responsibility of the State Sanitary Inspection. Monitoring of the commercial quality of food will be carried out by the Agricultural and Food Articles Trade Quality Inspection. In addition, the project involves a number of changes at the statutory level aimed at harmonising the tax authorities' approach to the forms of taxation carried out by farmers in the retail trade. This is to be served by introducing tax preferences for agricultural producers engaged in agricultural retail trade and by granting the Minister responsible for agriculture, in agreement with the Minister responsible for health, the competence to establish, by means of regulations, limits on the size of the activities carried out by one operator in the framework of agricultural retail trade and the hygiene requirements for those operators. Given that the proposed changes aim to create better opportunities for Polish farmers to develop their production and sales to final consumers of food produced in whole or in part from their own cultivation, farming or breeding, the Free and Solidarnia Circle supports the further process of this project. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u547	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I tried hard to write down the questions, maybe they don't sound exactly like that, but I'll try to answer them. Now, with regard to the draft Kukiz Act15, there are a lot of misunderstandings that result from the definition. The return on direct sales is used as a sale to the final consumer and to other retail establishments. And that is the fundamental difference, because direct sales mainly concern unprocessed food, as Mrs Niedziela has already said, and retail trade concerns the sale of processed and unprocessed food. In the case of direct sales referred to by Kukiz15, we are dealing with national rules that can be applied. On the other hand, this law also refers to processed foods for which mandatory production conditions are laid down in Union law. So, in general, there are a lot of misunderstandings, and it is us who, in the end, have created a definition of agricultural retail. That is the difference that agricultural retail trade will take into account processed and unprocessed food, but dedicated only to the final consumer. By the way, there was a question about group sales. In the case of agricultural retail trade, group sales are not envisaged. Such sales are taken into account for direct sales, so this will not be possible here. The project introduces a new type of active military service: territorial military service, which will be a permanent service as an active military service. [[Weekend.]] This service, in accordance with Article. 98 of the new law will be in two forms. First, as a rotational service as defined in the plan of the commander of the military unit at least two days a month. This will be the period of service of the military unit's structures and posts. Secondly, as a service available in parallel to the pursuit of work and civil life. It provides for participation in specialized courses, trainings, training courses for military habits and habits as well as self-education. The project introduces the principle of directing soldiers appointed for the first time to territorial military service for basic training of at least 16 days, after which they will take a military oath according to the general rota of the Polish Armed Forces, while the service period of the territorial defence soldiers itself will last from one to six years, with the possibility of extending for further periods. These soldiers will be given a wide range of opportunities for military professional development in the field of territorial defence, i.e. acquiring new skills, promoting and receiving awards. After 3 years of service they will gain priority, and this is a special privilege, in calling for candidate and professional military service. The project assumes priority in the appointment to the territorial military service of members of pro-defence organisations and graduates of profiled military classes. I have already stressed that the merit of this youth, which has led to the creation of a wide network of over a dozen thousand, in fact over a hundred thousand networks of defence structures and organizations and an even larger network of military classes, uniform military classes, is special. The project provides for the granting to soldiers of a monthly territorial defence allowance for their readiness to perform a territorial military service, to continuously improve their physical fitness and professional qualifications and to maintain their ability to act immediately in rotational form. It should be stressed that the territorial defence soldiers will be obliged to be in constant readiness for immediate service. The allowances in question shall be established by the Minister for National Defence by means of a special regulation. This amount is expected to amount to at least 10% for a serial. salary of a professional soldier, i.e. About 300,000. zł. In addition, soldiers will receive nearly PLN 200 for each part of the exercises and additions. In total, this is expected to mean more than 500 PLN for a soldier of professional territorial protection and for an officer of more than 600 PLN. The project also includes solutions that provide an opportunity to take up - this is an extremely important element, which must be mentioned - cooperation between the Territorial Defence Forces and existing paramilitary structures in Poland, such as the Railway Guard, the Forest Guard and the Fire Department. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u562	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz club15 on the amendment of the Act on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland. The main objective of the proposed amendment is to create the Territorial Defence Army as another independent type of Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. What's OT? It is a component of a modern military structure, whose connection to the non-military system decisively affects the effectiveness of national defence. The emergence of universal territorial defence is the most important task in building a sovereign, independent and invincible Republic. This rank of territorial defence is directly due to its decisive role in ensuring an effective defence of the State. In recent years, Poland's military organization has been largely destroyed. This is demonstrated by the miniaturisation of operational troops, the waiver of general military training of citizens and the creation of personal reserves for the development of mobilization, the distortion of hierarchicality and uniformity of the management and command system, and finally the complete elimination of the foundations of universal territorial defence built in 1991-2005. Today, Poland faces serious geopolitical challenges that have an important impact on its security. We are observing Russia's return to the armed reconstruction of the empire. This is accompanied by the helplessness and helplessness of Western European countries. The geopolitical situation indicates that the rapid reconstruction of a universal territorial defence providing effective defence and creating convenient conditions for maneuvering and striving for their own operational forces should be considered as a vital challenge for Polish security. The draft amendment to the General Defence Obligation Act under discussion of the High House provides for the creation of OT troops as a separate and independent type of Armed Forces, together with the appointment of the commander of these troops, a commander equivalent to the operational commander and general commander of types of Armed Forces. On the one hand, This is justified because by the lack of direct submission to operational forces, the independence of the Territorial Defence Forces is ensured for their effectiveness. On the other hand, however, this solution is a logical inconsistency, because OT is introduced as a new kind of Armed Forces, but at the same time it does not align the position of their commander with the position of persons directing the other types of Armed Forces, i.e. according to the current law so-called. Chiefs of Inspectors. This is, of course, a consequence of the disruption of the uniformity and hierarchicality of the Armed Forces' management and command system and of the lack of corrective action that has been announced by the current government for a long time. The project introduces a new title of soldier OT. The term is to function in parallel to the title of soldier given to persons serving in other types of Armed Forces. A new category is also introduced, i.e. territorial military service, which is to function in parallel to the category of military service relating to persons serving in other types of Armed Forces. However, such a solution should be considered rather curiosical, because if we consider that the Armed Forces are and should be a unified structure with a hierarchical chain of command, the creation of other parallel beings proves at least a lack of responsibility. We believe, however, that it would be much better to use existing legal institutions and make the desired changes based on the reserve soldier category. There are also doubts about the introduction of restrictions for OT soldiers on public rights and freedoms regarding their membership of associations. It seems that such a solution is absolutely unnecessary and contradicts the civic character of the service. However, what bothers the most is that the draft amendment lacks the restoration of universal military training. In the face of the challenges and difficult geopolitical situation of Poland, each month of continuing the shameful decision of the predecessors to suspend universal military service weakens our ability to carry out effective mobilization and reduces our personal reserve system. The draft amendment also lacks legal solutions to exploit the potential of pro-defence organisations in the OT system. After all, these organisations have often called for a uniform system of training and certification allowing for the inclusion in the territorial defence of entire divisions of pro-defence organisations. Unfortunately, the designers also lacked the courage to allow the Territorial Defence Forces, [[♪ The bell ♪]] Soldiers holding guns for personal use at home... [[Voice from the room: Time.]] ...as it is in the case of collectors or sports shooters. [[Voice from the room: Time.]] Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Although the proposed amendment has a number of weaknesses, the creation of territorial defence cannot be for reasons of Poland's security...
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u563	Finish it, Member.

In addition, it should be taken into account that in the war strategies of advanced countries there is a radical transformation in military techniques, e.g. The American Big Five armor program, which included Abrams Tank, Bradley Fighting Vehicle, Patriot Air Defence Missile System, Apache Helicopter, Black Hawk Helicopter, collapsed in the late 1980s. In 1990 a new Army Transformation Program was launched in the United States, where Unmanned Aerial Vehicles were given priority, that is, drones. It seems that territorial defence is well suited to developing dynamically dispersed military systems based on remotely controlled slots and advanced information technology.
