ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u639	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party our position on the report of the Senate's resolution on the Act amending certain laws laying down the conditions for conducting innovative activities. Today's tax law package, law on the rules of exercising the powers of the State Treasury, the Law on Higher Education and the Act on the Polish Academy of Sciences is aimed at dynamicising the innovative activities of Polish research centres and improving the conditions of innovative activity in our country. For years Poland has been in the tail of global innovation rankings, while our universities have been in the fourth hundred similar combinations. Despite the visible improvement in recent years, despite the promotion in innovation rankings, the current state of Polish science certainly does not exhaust our dreams and ambitions in this regard. The scientific community in Poland has been proposing tax changes in innovative activities for years. This law concerns amendments to both income tax laws and to the Tax Order. They consist of: abolishing the tax on intellectual and industrial property imports, extending the list of eligible costs to the costs of obtaining a patent, increasing the amount of eligible costs related to R &amp; D expenditure (research and development), which can be deducted from the tax base, extending the period foreseen for the deduction of costs associated with R &amp; D activities, increasing the amount of tax relief for taxable persons increasing the level of R &amp; D expenditure, the possibility of granting a cash refund for eligible expenditure which could not be granted because of the lack of income or the insufficient income to be taxed, treated on an equal footing with overpayment of tax. All these changes should be assessed positively. The subsequent amendments proposed by the authors of the Act concern the Law of 8 August 1996 on the rules for exercising the powers of the State Treasury. In the Article provision. 5a the amended act is added paragraph. la, which is to be introduced in respect of legal activities carried out by a public university, the Polish Academy of Sciences and its research institute, the requirement to notify the Treasury Minister if the market value of the subject-matter of the regulation exceeds the equivalent in PLN 250 000 euro. To date, this limit has been EUR 50,000. The amendment also provides for more flexibility in the rules for public legal entities having infrastructure, including research. This means that e.g. universities, research institutes, the Polish Academy of Sciences and its scientific institutes without the permission of the Minister of the Treasury will be able to conclude short, paid leases and leases, e.g. conference room, audiovisual equipment. In practice, they will be able to dispose of their assets more freely. Undoubtedly, the proposed direction of change is right, it should improve the condition of state research centres and, above all, their independence. Among tax changes, the Act provides, among others, for tax changes. the abolition (since January 2017) of the intellectual property income tax paid to the company. It also assumes the possibility for small and medium-sized enterprises to deduct from tax the costs of obtaining a patent and to increase the eligible costs of R &amp; D expenditure that can be deducted from the tax. For micro, small and medium-sized enterprises, these deductions will be up to 50%. Large companies will be able to deduct 50% personnel expenditure and 30% other costs related to R &amp; D activities (R &amp; D). In addition, the Act extends from the current 3 years to 6 years the period during which the trader can deduct the costs incurred for R &amp; D activities. A cash refund is also foreseen for new start-ups (so-called: start-ups) carrying out R &amp; D activities of 18% or 19% a potential but unused deduction. The Act discussed today by the High Chamber also affects the Law on Higher Education and the Act on the Polish Academy of Sciences. The first amendment concerns Article. Article 66 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Point 1, according to which the rector, as an institution of the university, makes decisions on its property and economy, as has been the case so far, up to the amount unchanged, i.e. EUR 250 000. There is also scope for commercialisation and for the contribution of the university to the company exemption from the provisions of the Law on the Rules of Enforcement of the Powers of the State Treasury (designed art. 86ba). Moreover, even after the creation of the company, the rector can recapitalise it with money. The projector imposes an obligation on the university employee to initiate the commercialisation process by providing information and a statement of interest in the transfer of rights to research expenditure. In this way, the proposed solution disciplines the worker on the initiation of the commercialisation process and, at the same time, obliges him to determine the direction of the action taken. As is currently the case, the decision whether or not to do so remains the authority of the university. The time available to the employee to participate in commercialisation benefits will also change. So far, there's been a five-year deadline. Instead, an unlimited share is proposed. The Polish Academy of Sciences is to gain greater independence in the disposal of its assets and investment activities. Several amendments were made to the draft amendment during the work of the Parliamentary Committees. One of them removes the provisions that gave rise to the possibility that the R &amp; D tax relief constitutes authorised State aid. Another amendment concerns grants granted to scientific bodies for the maintenance of research capacity. 2% These funds will have to be allocated to the financing of activities related to the commercialisation of research and development results. Previously, the project proposed that this should be 0.5%. Another amendment provides for the possibility of supporting Polish environmental technologies (in Poland and abroad) by the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. This amendment is related to the implementation of the NIK audit recommendations. These changes shorten the process of technology transfer, give greater autonomy to research institutes and allow researchers to participate more in the commercialisation of innovation. These changes are undoubtedly worthy of support. In conclusion, the Act introduces a coherent system of instruments to reward and encourage innovative activities. So-called. A small innovation law did not raise controversy in the High House. We worked on it in harmony, complementing and perfecting its project together. I would like to express my hope that also a broader package of changes, i.e. the so-called. a large innovative law will be conducted in a similar, substantive atmosphere, and above all will meet the expectations of scientific and business communities interested in R &amp; D. We are counting on the draft to be held by the Sejm soon. The Act adopted unanimously by the High House returns today after the adoption of the Senate amendments to the proceedings of the High Chamber. After reading the amendments, PSL maintains its previously expressed support for the Act amending certain laws setting out the conditions for carrying out innovative activities.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u645	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Well, in October this year. The 150th birthday anniversary and 80th anniversary of the death of the outstanding Polish politician Ignacy Daszyński passed. Ignacy Daszyński was born on October 26, 1866 in Zbaraz. He was one of the organizers of the Workers' Party in Lviv in 1890 and then established in 1892. The Galician Social Democratic Party, transformed in 1897 into the Polish Social-Democratic Party of Galicia and Silesia Cieszyński. Since 1893 he has edited the party press body. In the years 1897-1918 he was a Member of the Austrian Parliament, where he became famous as one of the best speakers stigmatizing the abuse of the authorities, corruption and exploitation. Ignacy Daszyński has been cooperating with the Polish Socialist Party since its creation. In 1901 he made close contact with Józef Piłsudski. During the 1905 revolution, he published an open letter to the CKR PPS, where the leader of the PPS criticized the Polish labour movement too closely with the Russian movement. He believed that it was the duty of the Polish Revolution to set its own goal: the independent Polish republic. Between 1912 and 1914 Daszyński was one of the leaders of the Interim Commission of Confederate Independence Parties. World War I began in 1914. Daszyński was a supporter of the participation of Poles in the war against Russia, on the side of Austro-Hungary. He called for the union of Galicia with the Kingdom of Poland and their transformation into the third part of the Habsburg monarchy, alongside Austria and Hungary. Ignacy Daszyński was the initiator of the creation and then the deputy president of the National Committee. At the end of World War I local power centers were established on Polish lands. On 19 October 1918, the National Council of the Duchy of Cieszyn was established in Cieszyn. On 28 October that year, Polish Liquidation Commission was established in Krakow. On the night of 6 to 7 November 1918, the Interim Government of the People's Republic of Poland was established in Lublin, where Ignacy Daszyński served as Prime Minister and Minister of Foreign Affairs. It was the first Polish government on land liberated from the rule of the invaders. The manifesto of this government was the most radical of the non-revolutionary concepts of the interwar 20th anniversary. He decided on the republican shape of the reborn Poland, introduced the principles of parliamentary democracy and decided on the freedom character of later legislation guaranteeing a significant range of civil liberties. The Interim Government of the People's Republic of Poland existed until 11 November 1918 when Józef Piłsudski was dissolved at the request of the arrival from Magdeburg. [[On 11 November we celebrate the Feast of Independence of Poland reborn after years of captivity. At that time it was a joyful day for all Poles. Ignacy Daszyński said: "The dreams of youth have come true. After years of war torture, suffering, fights, mistakes and hard work, remembered work, after so many transitions, after so many falls of hope, this is the independent Polish state that emerged from the bloody confusion of history. The feelings that spread my breast on that first day of freedom can only be compared with those that I felt in the hot days of the summer of 1914 at the beginning of the war. Not every generation can be proud of the experience of such epochal days.]] Between 1920 and 1921 he became Deputy Prime Minister in the so-called. The government of Wincent Witos National Defense. Between 1919 and 1930 he served as Member of the Sejm, in 1922 and 1927 he was Deputy Marshal, and in 1928 and 1930 he was Marshal of the Sejm. Because of his illness, he has not been active in political life since 1930. Ignacy Daszyński died [[♪ The bell ♪]] In Bystrą Śląska on October 31, 1936, a coffin with a body was exhibited in the Workers' House in Bielsk, where after a few days they were transported to Krakow and buried in the Rakowicki cemetery. Ignacy Daszyński has always combined his electoral programme with the fight for independence of Poland. In his international opinion, he strengthened his conviction that Poles had unprecedented rights to have an independent state. He manifested his dislike for all radical phenomena in the revolutionary movement, as well as for the Russian labour movement and the Russian revolution. He critically assessed the experience of the Soviet building of socialism. He wrote: What in 1906 was a magical program of the magnificent, huge, united Russian Republic soon became a terror of the civilized world. Daszyński tried to prove that the dictatorship of the proletariat in Polish conditions is not possible, and the only social programme is the programme of the PPS party justified by the words: the socialist system cannot be implemented against the will of the majority of society, so it must be based on democratic principles. I myself am quite distant from socialist ideas, while all those who voted for independent Poland deserve recognition and memory of the Polish people. Hello, Ignacy Daszyński's memory. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u647	Statement on the 210th anniversary of Emilie Plater's birth. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! November 13th this year The 210th anniversary of Emilie Plater's birth - a noblewoman who fought in the November Uprising. I would like to take this opportunity to say a few words about this patriotic legend. Emilia Plater was born on 13 November 1806 in Vilnius. She came from a family whose majority of representatives were great patriots and tied her life to the Polish cause. Emilia was a couple of years old when her parents split up. She then left with her mother, belonging to the relatives of Liksna's estate near Dyneburg, now Latvia. She was raised and educated together with the boys. She was interested in literature and history, she wrote poems, and she also rode horses, practiced fetish and hunted. She read a lot. Her heroes became Joan of Arc and the Greek of Bubulin. Emilia's historical interest in her personality was determined by her development. She supported the patriotic movement at that time and was delighted with the works of the great romantics Goethe and Schiller. She also showed great interest in the rural people. For patriotic reasons, she rejected the proposal of a wealthy Russian engineer. In 1830, she had a hard time surviving her mother's death. The news of the uprising called for a fight in Lithuania, but as a woman she was removed from the decision. After obtaining the promise of support from the students of the local school in Dyneburg, she developed a plan to conquer the city. The chance arose when in March 1831 Juliusz Grużewski's branch defeated Russians stationed in the village of Rosień, which became a signal for the uprising in Lithuania. Plater cut her hair and armored, in a men's outfit, together with her friend Maria Prószynska, she formed a several hundred-member insurgent unit, which included shooters, cavalrymen and scythesmen. On her way to Dyneburg, she gathered more volunteers and fought several victorious fights with the Russian forces. However, against the advantage of the opponent, she did not decide to attack the city, and her decimated unit joined the insurgent formation led by her relative. Emilia herself began to enjoy the sympathy and respect of the soldiers who appreciated her valor. In the spring of 1831 Plater joined Karol Załuski's unit at Ponowiewiec. Together with him she entered Wiłkomierz, where she met a new companion, Maria Raszanowicza. Together they participated in the skirmishes at Mishagoła and Garbielowo, where General Dezydera Chłapowski awarded Emilia the honorary rank of captain. His decision was also given to First Company Command 1. The regiment of the infantry of Lithuania. Plater was extremely valiant, defending the escorted troops to supply the insurgent army. When General Chłapowski, who was also in charge of her group, decided not to fight further, Plater decided to join the insurgent forces in the Warsaw area. Together with her relative Caesar Plater and Raszanowiczówna, she broke through the woods in her peasant costume, but the journey turned out to be too exhausting for her and the sick Emilia had to stop under the assumed name of Koravinska in Justianów at the Ablamowicz manor where she was treated. However, the treatment did not help and Emilia Plater died on 23 December 1831. Her body rested on the Ablamovich cemetery in Kopciów, today's Lithuania. The tomb was preserved until today, and Emilia Plater herself became a symbol of patriotism and the struggle for independence and the hero of poems. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u649	Curly roads leading to the UNESCO list. Tarnowskie Góry have two flagship monuments: silver mine (available for tourists since 1976) and Black trout Valley (this was made available in 1957). Both monuments appear on the presidential list of historical monuments. At the end of the 18th century, the state mine Fryderyk was launched, which operated in the years 1784 and 1912. It is a unique treasure of national heritage with preserved, very good condition, excavations mined with drainage chambers system. The Tarnogórska Land Lovers' Association has been fighting for years to include a valuable monument on the UNESCO list. To this day, a titanium work has been carried out to find itself on this prestigious world list. But before it comes true, it is worth recalling that success has many fathers, and failure usually one. That is why today, the PO-RAŚ team of local authorities of the Silesian Voivodeship wants to join the sailing ship. As in the parable, something attached to the ship and says that we are sailing. Ad rem: in 2012 the thought and desire to appear on the UNESCO list was born in Tarnowskie Góra. At that time, one of the members of the Management Board of the Silesian Voivodeship appointed a team of experts to select specific monuments and to recommend them. Among other things, they were included in the team. Dariusz Walerjański, director of the International Centre for Documentation and Research on Industrial Heritage for Tourism in Zabrze, Leszek Jodliński, then director of the Silesian Museum, as well as Jacek Owczarek from the Silesian Centre for Cultural Heritage. Then it was decided, among others. to the amazement of the Tarnogórska Land Lovers' Association, that the list recommended radio station in Gliwice and the monuments of coal mining in Zabrze, but: it will not be received (recommendation) by the silver mine in Tarnowskie Góry, where it turns out that it is not known who owns underground excavations. It is important for UNESCO to ensure a bright future for the object. In all these three cases, it is not known what it will be...," said the President's Gazet Wyborcza, a member of the board of directors of Jerzy Gorzelik (quotat za: http://katowice. wyborcza. == sync, corrected by elderman == @elder_man html#ixzz1oY9GgXCc h). ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u650	Declaration on the occasion of the 65th anniversary of the Polish Union of the Blinds in the Service of Generations and the 70th anniversary of the Unification of the Movement of Blind People in Poland. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! This year there is a wonderful anniversary of a unique group, such as blind and visually impaired people in Poland. It is the anniversary of the 65th anniversary of the Polish Union of the Blindness in the Service of Generations and the 70th anniversary of the Unification of the Movement of Blind People in Poland. The Jubilee was under the honorary patronage of the President of the Republic of Poland Andrzej Duda. I would like to pay due respect to the union activists, their selfless involvement and admirable energy. A blind relationship helps blind people and their loved ones to find themselves in a new situation resulting from loss of vision. It teaches you how to be independent at home, travel, work and how to actively participate in social life. It works for systemic solutions to facilitate the functioning of people with visual impairment and ensures that they are properly perceived. I personally support the District of Opole of the Polish Blind Union and the Circle of the Polish Blind Union in Namysłów since I was still vice-stars, and I will continue to do so. I would like to express my special appreciation to Mr Włodzimierz Ożga, the director of the Opolskie District of PZN and to the president of the circle in Namysłów Krystyna Pacholik and all members of the organization. Your work and commitment are of fundamental social importance to the environment of blind and visually impaired people in Namysłów and Opole region. I wish you further success and satisfaction from your work for the years to come and more wonderful anniversaries. I cordially greet you from the Sejm of the Republic of Poland.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-03-sejm-29-1.u651	Statement on the celebration of the anniversary of the 1050th anniversary of Poland's baptism in the Puławy district. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! 2016 is a unique year for our Church, state and nation. This year because we celebrate the anniversary of the 1050th anniversary of Poland's baptism. These most important celebrations took place on April 14 and 15 in Gniezno and Poznań. The first day of the Polish Episcopal Conference and Holy Mass took place. Thanksgiving in the Cathedral of Gniezno. In Poznań, in turn, there were ceremonial debates of the National Assembly, i.e. the united Chambers of the Sejm and the Senate; the message was delivered by the President of the Republic of Poland, Andrzej Duda. Then there was Mass. in the Poznań Cathedral, in Ostrów Tumski with the participation of the Polish Episcopate, representatives of European Episcopates, the President of the Republic of Poland and parliamentarians. The President of the Republic of Poland laid flowers on the grave of I and Bolesław Chrobre in the Golden Chapel. Its celebrations summarizing the anniversary of the 1050th anniversary of Poland's baptism also had a community of Puławy County, from which I come. They took place on Friday 28 October 2016 in Puławy. The celebrations began in the Puławy Cultural Centre's Showroom. Representatives of authorities and central, local and local authorities, uniformed services, educational, cultural units, as well as companies from the Puławy district participated in this uplifting event. A number of communal delegations added the special character of the ceremony to the flag of the county and the coat of arms of all municipalities. The session was held by the President of the Puławy District Council Danuta Smaga. Mr Krzysztof Szulowski, Deputy Marshal of the Lublin Voivodeship, Mr Grzegorz Kawiet, the commune of Puławy Krzysztof Brzeziński and the Puławy Starosta Witold Ashes. Personally in my speech I emphasized the importance of Poland's baptism for state formation. The following part of the ceremony presents the most important projects carried out by the organizational units of the county and municipalities, as well as associations and other entities which were connected with the anniversary of the 1050th anniversary of Poland's baptism. The ceremony was complemented by a lecture by historian Dr. Hab. Tomisława Girgiel devoted to the genesis and socio-cultural consequences of Poland's baptism. The event ended with the performance of the choir of the municipality of Kurów. At the end of the official part, the participants of the celebration went together in the solemn origins of the flag mail assist to the church of St. Our Lady of the Rosary, where the Holy Mass took place. chaired by Fr. Archbishop Stanisław Budzik, Lublin Metropolitan. A special point of the Mass was the act of giving the care of Our Lady of Puławy County by the chairman of the District Council of Danuta Smaga and the governor of Puławy Witold Ashes. All those involved in social, political, professional activities, working for the Puławy District, on a daily basis differing views, fighting conflict and political wars, I wish that the idea of Christian Poland and the nation drawing from the 105-year-old heritage be values that unite us all. Thank you.
